People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Um, hi. Thank you for seeing me. I, um... I guess I'm just here because I've been feeling a little overwhelmed lately. It's probably nothing major, I mean, I know everyone gets stressed, but... I just can't seem to shake this feeling that everything going wrong is somehow my fault. 

Like, at work, I keep thinking I'm just a terrible employee, and then at home, I feel like I'm failing there too. Sometimes I even think I deserve to lose everything I've worked so hard for. I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds really silly and dramatic, doesn't it? I just... I want to be doing a good job, and I feel like I'm letting everyone down. Is it okay that I'm bringing this up?

Um... well, I guess I've been a little less active lately. I used to love planning brunches with my friends, or, you know, going into the spare room to paint. I even went hiking a lot and signed up for all these networking events for work.

I still try to do them so I don't let anyone down, but the spark is kind of missing. 

I'm sorry, I know I should be more appreciative of having hobbies and friends to do things with. Does that sound like what you were asking? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.

Um... I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I haven't been actively trying to lose weight or anything, and I know I should be taking better care of myself. I hope it doesn't sound like I'm neglecting my health on purpose.

Um... I think I might have mentioned this just a second ago, but... yeah, I guess I really don't get the same feeling out of things anymore. I'm sorry if I'm repeating myself, I just want to make sure I'm answering you right.

Um... well, it's been a little while, I guess. I mean, I don't want to make it sound like I've just been sitting around feeling sorry for myself this whole time, because I really have been trying to push through and keep up with everything.

I just kept hoping I could snap out of it on my own, you know? 

Is that... is that the right kind of timeline you were looking for? I hope it doesn't make me seem like I haven't been trying to get better.

Um... yeah, I guess I do. I mean, I try really hard not to let it get to me, because I know I should just be grateful for what I have. I don't want to sound unappreciative or like I'm complaining too much.

I mean, I try really hard not to let it get to me, because I know I should just be grateful for what I have. I don't want to sound unappreciative or like I'm complaining too much. \n\nBut honestly, that's kind of a big part of why I finally came in to see someone. I just... I can't stop feeling like every little thing that goes wrong is entirely my fault. Like, if a project gets delayed at work, or if a pipe leaks at my house, my brain just immediately goes to, \"I'm a terrible employee,\" or \"I'm a horrible homeowner.\" It feels like I just deserve to lose everything I've worked so hard for because I'm not doing a good enough job keeping it all together. \n\nI know it probably sounds silly to get so worked up over things I can't always control. It just makes me feel like I deserve to lose everything I've worked for, even when I know that's probably an irrational way to look at it. \n\nI know it's not a very productive way to think, and I promise I really am trying to be kinder to myself. Am I... am I giving you the right kind of examples? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless or have a significantly lowered opinion of yourself compared to before you started feeling overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I suppose I do. It just feels like I'm not worth it anymore. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that sounds so dramatic, and I really am trying to snap out of it and be more confident again. Um... well, it's been a little while. I really don't want to sound like I've just been wallowing in it or anything, because I've honestly been trying my best to push through and keep up with my job and everything else.

I just kept hoping I could fix it myself and be back to normal without bothering anyone.

I just can't seem to shake this feeling that everything going wrong is somehow my fault. \n\nLike, at work, I keep thinking I'm just a terrible employee, and then at home, I feel like I'm failing there too. Sometimes I even think I deserve to lose everything I've worked so hard for. I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds really silly and dramatic, doesn't it? I just... I want to be doing a good job, and I feel like I'm letting everyone down. I mean, I have a good job and a nice life, so I really have no reason to be sad, right? I'd hate to sound ungrateful or like I'm just complaining over nothing. \n\nI haven't really noticed an empty feeling, either, I don't think. I mostly just worry about messing things up and letting people down. Is that... does that answer your question okay? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do lately?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess I've been a little less active lately. I used to love planning brunches with my friends, or, you know, going into the spare room to paint. I even went hiking a lot and signed up for all these networking events for work. \n\nBut lately... I don't know, I guess I just haven't been as into it? It's not that I don't like those things anymore, I just... when I do them now, they feel more like tasks I'm just pretending to care about, if that makes sense. I still try to do them so I don't let anyone down, but the spark is kind of missing. \n\nI'm sorry, I know I should be more appreciative of having hobbies and friends to do things with. I think I've mostly just been living on coffee, maybe some toast, or whatever is easiest to grab so I don't waste time. \n\nI know I really need to do better with cooking proper meals. Is that... does that answer your question? I hope it's okay that I haven't been keeping very good track of the exact numbers on the scale.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're no longer able to experience pleasure from activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... I think I might have mentioned this just a second ago, but... yeah, I guess I really don't get the same feeling out of things anymore. I'm sorry if I'm repeating myself, I just want to make sure I'm answering you right.\n\nLike I said, I used to love planning those weekend brunches, or going into the spare room to paint, and even hiking and going to all those networking events for work. I really did enjoy them. But now... it's just really hard to find that feeling. When I do those things now, it feels more like I'm just going through the motions, like they're chores I'm just pretending to care about so I don't upset anyone or seem lazy. \n\nI know it sounds awful to say that about spending time with friends or doing nice things, and I really am trying to be better about it. I just don't feel that spark of happiness from them right now. Does that... is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's been a little while, I guess. Roughly. I'm sorry, I know that sounds like a really long time to let something go without fixing it, and I probably should have said something sooner. I just kept hoping I could snap out of it on my own, you know? \n\nIs that... is that the right kind of timeline you were looking for? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... yes, actually! I really want to make sure I'm giving you the whole picture, and it hasn't been completely bad the entire time. There was actually a stretch of, oh, maybe several weeks where that heavy feeling lifted a bit. I was getting through work a lot more normally, which I was really happy about because I hate falling behind or being a burden to my team. 

But... um... I don't know if I should even mention this next part. I really don't want to sound crazy or make you think worse of me. But even during those weeks when my mood was better and I didn't feel so worthless... I, um... I still heard that voice near the air vents. And I was still completely convinced that the mortgage company and my coworkers were monitoring me. 

I'm so sorry, I know that sounds really strange and I probably shouldn't have let it go on without saying anything. I just want to be totally honest with you so you have all the facts to do your job. Did... did I explain that okay? I hope that's the kind of information you were looking for.

I just want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture without sounding too dramatic.\n\nDoctor: Are there any periods of time, even if just a few days, where you don't feel worthless at all?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, actually! I really want to make sure I'm giving you the whole picture, and it hasn't been completely bad the entire time. There was actually a stretch of, oh, maybe several weeks where that heavy feeling lifted a bit. I was getting through work a lot more normally, which I was really happy about because I hate falling behind or being a burden to my team. \n\nBut... um... People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, yes, like I mentioned just a bit ago, there was that one period. I really do try to hold onto those times to remind myself that things aren't always so dark. For a few weeks, my mood wasn't nearly as heavy, and I was actually getting through my work day pretty normally, which was such a relief because I hate feeling like I'm letting my team down.

But... um, I guess I should reiterate... even when I was feeling a bit more worthy and capable during those weeks, the other stuff didn't stop. I still... I still heard that voice by the air vents. And I was still just as certain that the mortgage company and the people at work were keeping tabs on me. 

I'm so sorry to keep bringing that up, I know it probably sounds so bizarre and I really don't want you to think I'm completely losing my mind. I just want to make sure I'm giving you all the right details. Is... is it okay that I'm explaining it this way? I just want to be a good patient and give you what you need.

When I do those things now, it feels more like I'm just going through the motions, like they're chores I'm just pretending to care about so I don't upset anyone or seem lazy. \n\nI know it sounds awful to say that about spending time with friends or doing nice things, and I really am trying to be better about it. I just don't feel that spark of happiness from them right now. Does that... is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's been a little while, I guess. I mean, I don't want to make it sound like I've just been sitting around feeling sorry for myself this whole time, because I really have been trying to push through and keep up with everything. \n\nBut I suppose... if I really think about it, this feeling—the sadness and just not caring about my hobbies as much—it's been going on for about two years and one month. Roughly. I'm sorry, I know that sounds like a really long time to let something go without fixing it, and I probably should have said something sooner. I just kept hoping I could snap out of it on my own, you know? \n\nIs that... is that the right kind of timeline you were looking for? I hope it doesn't make me seem like I haven't been trying to get better.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess I do. I mean, I try really hard not to let it get to me, because I know I should just be grateful for what I have. I don't want to sound unappreciative or like I'm complaining too much. \n\nBut honestly, that's kind of a big part of why I finally came in to see someone. I just... I can't stop feeling like every little thing that goes wrong is entirely my fault. Like, if a project gets delayed at work, or if a pipe leaks at my house, my brain just immediately goes to, \"I'm a terrible employee,\" or \"I'm a horrible homeowner.\" It feels like I just deserve to lose everything I've worked so hard for because I'm not doing a good enough job keeping it all together. \n\nI know it probably sounds silly to get so worked up over things I can't always control. I really am trying to be more positive, I promise. Does that... make sense? I hope I'm not overreacting by feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often criticize yourself for things that might not even be your fault?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yes, definitely. I think that ties right into what I was just rambling about a second ago... I'm so sorry, I hope I'm not repeating myself too much or talking in circles! \n\nBut yes, I am really hard on myself. Like I mentioned with the work delays or the house... even if I logically know I didn't personally cause a pipe to burst, I still criticize myself over it. My brain just twists it into being my fault, and I end up telling myself I'm a bad homeowner or a bad employee. It just makes me feel like I deserve to lose everything I've worked for, even when I know that's probably an irrational way to look at it. \n\nI know it's not a very productive way to think, and I promise I really am trying to be kinder to myself. I know I have a lot of good things going for me, and I try to focus on that. \n\nBut... yeah, I think my opinion of myself has definitely dropped since this all started. It's like I was saying before... that feeling of worthlessness is honestly the main reason I finally made this appointment. I just can't shake this awful feeling that I'm just fundamentally failing at everything. Like I'm a terrible employee, and a terrible homeowner... and that because of that, I just don't deserve any of the things I've worked so hard for. I guess the depression, and just... losing interest in things I normally like... it's been with me for about two years and one month now. Give or take a little?
